<artifact_plan>
id: gen_plan_experiment_1_idx1
type: experiment
title: 'VUCCA: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Conformal Cache Admission'
summary: >-
  Implement the fully corrected VUCCA method featuring Adaptive Conformal Inference (ACI), soft-sigmoid admission bias, and
  fine-grained semantic clustering, evaluated across stationary, shift, and cold-start regimes against LRU, TinyLFU, and ARC2.
runpod_compute_profile: cpu_light
implementation_pseudocode: "import numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.cluster import KMeans\nfrom scipy.special import expit as sigmoid\n\
  \n# --- Components ---\nclass CacheSimulator:\n    # Implements LRU, TinyLFU, ARC2\n    # TinyLFU uses a Count-Min Sketch\
  \ for frequency tracking\n    # ARC2 maintains T1, T2, B1, B2 lists\n\nclass VUCCAAdmission:\n    def __init__(self, tau_base=0.3,\
  \ beta=0.4, gamma=5.0, alpha=0.1, lambda_aci=0.01):\n        self.tau_base = tau_base\n        self.beta = beta\n      \
  \  self.gamma = gamma\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.lambda_aci = lambda_aci\n        self.cluster_stats = {}\n\
  \        self.quantiles = {}\n\n    def get_bias(self, cluster_id):\n        # Soft-Sigmoid: tau_c = tau_base + beta * sigmoid(gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n        u_c = self.cluster_stats[cluster_id]['half_width']\n        return self.tau_base + self.beta * sigmoid(self.gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n\n    def update_conformal(self, cluster_id, actual_miss_rate, predicted_miss_rate, timestamp, regime):\n\
  \        s = abs(predicted_miss_rate - actual_miss_rate)\n        if regime == 'shift':\n            # ACI: Exponentially\
  \ weighted update\n            # weight = exp(-lambda * (current_t - t_i))\n            # Update weighted quantile q_hat\n\
  \            pass\n        else:\n            # Split Conformal: Add to calibration set, compute quantile of s\n       \
  \     pass\n\n# --- Main Experiment Loop ---\n# 1. Load datasets from art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y (stationary, shift, cold_start)\n\
  # 2. Feature Enhancement (Optional):\n#    If budget allows, call OpenRouter to get CLIP embeddings for sample IDs\n#  \
  \  Else use provided 512-d embeddings\n# 3. Clustering:\n#    Run KMeans(K=64) -> Merge clusters with < 5 elements into\
  \ nearest neighbor\n# 4. Simulation Logic:\n#    for regime in regimes:\n#        for chunk in chunks:\n#            # Split\
  \ chunk: 20% Calibration, 80% Test\n#            cal_data, test_data = split(chunk)\n#            \n#            # Step\
  \ A: Calibration\n#            for item in cal_data:\n#                # Update miss rate estimates and conformal quantiles\n\
  #                vucca.update_conformal(item.cluster, ...)\n#            \n#            # Step B: Execution\n#         \
  \   for request in test_data:\n#                if request.item not in cache:\n#                    tau_c = vucca.get_bias(request.cluster)\n\
  #                    if admission_filter(request, tau_c):\n#                        cache.admit(request.item)\n#       \
  \         else:\n#                    cache.hit()\n#\n# 5. Ablations:\n#    - Replace soft-sigmoid with exp(u_c)\n#    -\
  \ Replace ACI with standard Split Conformal in 'shift' regime\n#    - Change K=64 to K=10\n#    - Replace Conformal Interval\
  \ Width with Entropy-based uncertainty\n#\n# 6. Metrics Calculation:\n#    - Hit Ratio = Hits / Total Requests\n#    - Throughput\
  \ = (Hits * Hit_Latency + Misses * Miss_Latency) / Total_Time\n#    - Coverage = count(actual_miss_rate in [M-q, M+q]) /\
  \ total_samples"
fallback_plan: >-
  If ACI convergence is too slow or unstable, fallback to a sliding-window Conformal Prediction where the calibration set
  is the last N requests. If the soft-sigmoid bias doesn't show significant improvement over baselines, test a piecewise-linear
  bias function. If CLIP API costs exceed $5, rely entirely on the synthetic 512-d embeddings provided in the dataset, as
  they are already designed to mimic semantic structure.
testing_plan: |-
  1. Mini-Run: Execute the pipeline on 1 chunk of the 'stationary' regime. Verify that hit rates for LRU/TinyLFU are consistent with known behaviors and that the conformal interval is effectively bounding the miss rate.
  2. Bias Verification: Print the `tau_c` values for high-confidence vs low-confidence clusters to ensure the soft-sigmoid is producing a gradient (e.g., 0.3 to 0.7) rather than binary values.
  3. Coverage Check: Calculate the percentage of actual miss rates that fall within the conformal intervals for the calibration set; it should be approximately 1-alpha (e.g., 90%).
  4. Regime Sanity: In the 'shift' regime, verify that ACI-based thresholds adapt faster than split-conformal thresholds after the popularity shift point.
</artifact_plan>

<dependencies>
Read the files in these dependency workspaces to understand what's available, then copy any you need into your working directory.

--- Dependency 1 ---
id: art_IuIwT_8rgp5S
type: research
title: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Cache Admission Survey
summary: >-
  Comprehensive survey across 20+ verified sources confirming: (A) 12 modern cache admission baselines identified with exact
  venues, mechanisms, and metrics — ZERO use conformal prediction or visual/semantic signals for admission; (B) Conformal
  prediction gap confirmed — no prior work applies distribution-free uncertainty quantification to cache admission, with the
  closest analogs being DEAP Cache's KDE (distribution modeling, not conformal) and Guard's robustness bounds (algorithmic,
  not statistical); (C) Visual/semantic embeddings used for similarity search in caching but never combined with uncertainty-aware
  admission; (D) Standard block traces lack semantic signal, requiring object/CDN traces (S3-FIFO collection: 6,594 traces,
  856B requests) or synthetic embedding-object workloads; (E) Key risks: exchangeability violations requiring ACI, embedding
  inference overhead, and cold-start handling.
workspace_path: >-
  /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_XUVIPW24BQbP/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_research_1
out_dependency_files:

  - research_out.json

--- Dependency 2 ---
id: art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y
type: dataset
title: Cache Access Trace Datasets with Visual Embeddings
summary: >-
  This artifact provides three cache-access trace datasets for evaluating conformal cache admission policies. Each dataset
  pairs real-derived request patterns (based on Tencent CDN trace statistics) with 512-d visual embeddings and semantic cluster
  metadata. The three regimes are: (1) stationary_hot_clusters — stable Zipf-distributed popularity with a few dominant hot
  clusters, (2) sudden_popularity_shift — request mass migrates from old clusters to new ones midway through the sequence,
  and (3) cold_start — a stream of previously-unseen items with no warm-up period. Each dataset contains ~800-900 items with
  512-d embeddings, popularity counts, cluster IDs, timestamps, and temporal train/validation/test splits. Total of 2,578
  examples across 3 datasets. The request sequences are ordered by timestamp (no future leakage), enabling downstream experiments
  to replay TinyLFU, ARC2, LRU, and new conformal admission policies. No hit/miss labels or admission decisions are pre-computed;
  those are downstream experiment outputs. Full dataset is 25MB, with mini (9 examples) and preview (9 examples, truncated)
  variants for quick inspection.
workspace_path: >-
  /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_XUVIPW24BQbP/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1
out_dependency_files:
  file_list:
  - data.py
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json
  data_file_paths:
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json

Data files come in three sizes:
- preview_*_out.json — READ THIS to inspect the data structure
- mini_*_out.json (~3 examples) — use for prototyping/testing

Set every Bash timeout to fit within the budget.\n2. **Start small, scale up**: Run on minimal input first, fix errors, then increase scale.\n3. **Extrapolate before scaling**: Use recorded runtimes to predict whether the next step fits in the budget. Don't guess \u2014 calculate.\n4. **Background execution**: For anything that takes >1 min, run in background (`run_in_background=true`) and do useful work while waiting.\n5. **Stop early if needed**: Quality results on less data beats a timeout or crash. It's always acceptable to stop at a smaller scale.\n\n---\n\n## Gradual Scaling Sequence\n\nRun code at increasing data sizes, checking runtime at each step.\n\nSubstitute your actual file names:\n- `{mini_file}` \u2014 mini JSON (3 examples) from dependency workspace\n- `{full_file}` \u2014 full dataset from dependency workspace\n- `{script}` \u2014 your processing script (e.g., `./method.py`, `./eval.py`)\n- `{schema}` \u2014 JSON schema to validate output against\n\n**STEP 1 \u2014 MINI DATA:** Run `{script}` on `{mini_file}`. Do NOT truncate logs. Fix all errors. Validate output against `{schema}`. Verify you are NOT using mock scripts, mock data, or mock APIs.\n\n**STEP 2 \u2014 10 EXAMPLES:** Modify `{script}` to load only the first 10 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Validate schema. Record the runtime.\n\n**STEP 3 \u2014 50 EXAMPLES:** Load first 50 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Record runtime. **EXTRAPOLATE**: Using runtimes from steps 2-3, estimate time per example. Calculate how many examples fit in your remaining time budget. If 50 already used most of the budget, stop here.\n\n**STEP 4 \u2014 100 EXAMPLES (if budget allows):** Load first 100 examples. Run and fix errors. Record runtime. Re-extrapolate with the new data point.\n\n**STEP 5 \u2014 200 EXAMPLES (if budget allows):** Load first 200 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Record runtime.\n\n**STEP 6 \u2014 MAXIMIZE:** Using all recorded runtimes, extrapolate time-per-example (it may not be perfectly linear \u2014 account for overhead). Calculate the maximum number of examples that fits within your remaining time budget with a 10% safety margin. Load that many (or all if they fit). Run and validate.\n\n## Final Testing Phase\n\nAfter completing the scaling sequence, redo the entire sequence **one more time** up to your final example count:\n\nmini \u2192 10 \u2192 50 \u2192 100 \u2192 200 \u2192 max\n\nAt each scale: look for issues, fix problems, validate output, ensure it completes within time limits.\n\n---\n\n## Background Execution\n\nFor any step that takes >1 min, run as a **background task**:\n\n1. Launch with Bash `run_in_background=true`\n2. While it runs, use the time productively:\n   - Sanity-check previous outputs\n   - Verify file integrity (correct field names, non-empty values)\n   - Review code for edge cases at larger scale\n   - Prepare the next step\n3. Check back on the background task to get results\n4. If it failed, fix errors and re-run\n\n---\n\n## Resource Limits\n\nSet hard RAM and CPU time limits so code fails fast instead of crashing the system. Read limits from `<hardware>` and leave headroom for the OS (e.g., if 16GB total, cap at 14GB).\n\nPython example using stdlib `resource` module:\n```python\nimport resource\nresource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (14 * 1024**3, 14 * 1024**3))  # 14GB RAM\nresource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_CPU, (3600, 3600))  # 1 hour CPU time\n```\nExceeding RAM raises `MemoryError`. Exceeding CPU time sends `SIGKILL`.\n\n## Monitoring\n\nAt each step, record runtime AND check resource usage (`free -h` for RAM, `top -bn1 | head -5` for CPU). If memory usage is climbing toward the limit or CPU is pegged, stop and investigate before scaling further.\n\n---\nThis skill is located at `/ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-long-running-tasks`. {"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "**Step 1** \u2014 Run `bash scripts/get_hardware.sh` (relative to this skill's directory).\n\nRead the `=== CGROUP ===` section carefully. If `Type: cgroup v1` or `cgroup v2`:\n- You are in a **container with hard resource limits**. Exceeding them = OOM kill, no recovery.\n- **Never** use `psutil.virtual_memory().total`, `free -h`, `/proc/meminfo`, `os.cpu_count()`, or `nproc` for resource limits \u2014 these report **host** values, not your container's allocation.\n- **Always** read limits from the cgroup paths shown in the output, or use the Python helpers below.\n- For **runtime memory monitoring**, read current usage from cgroup too:\n  - v2: `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.current`\n  - v1: `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory/memory.usage_in_bytes`\n\n**Step 2** \u2014 Use Step 1 results to pick package variants **before** installing.\n\nDefaults often target the most powerful environment \u2014 PyPI's `torch` ships with CUDA libs even on CPU-only hosts. Wrong variant = wasted disk, slow setup, possible import-time failures.\n\nIf `=== GPU ===` shows `No GPU`, install torch's CPU build (skips ~4.5GB of CUDA libs):\n```bash\nuv pip install torch --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu\n```\nSame idea for any library whose wheel selection depends on detected hardware (GPU/CPU-only builds, architecture-specific wheels).\n\nAfter install, sanity-check imports right away (`python -c \"import torch\"`). Disk-pressure or interrupted installs leave half-built wheels (e.g. `libtorch_global_deps.so` missing) \u2014 catch these before the experiment runs.\n\n**Step 3** \u2014 Set Python constants from the Step 1 results:\n```python\nimport os, math, torch, psutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef _detect_cpus() -> int:\n    \"\"\"Detect actual CPU allocation (containers/pods/bare metal).\"\"\"\n    try:  # cgroups v2 quota\n        parts = Path(\"/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu.max\").read_text().split()\n        if parts[0] != \"max\":\n            return math.ceil(int(parts[0]) / int(parts[1]))\n    except (FileNotFoundError, ValueError): pass\n    try:  # cgroups v1 quota\n        q = int(Path(\"/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu/cpu.cfs_quota_us\").read_text())\n        p = int(Path(\"/sys/fs/cgroup/cpu/cpu.cfs_period_us\").read_text())\n        if q > 0:\n            return math.ceil(q / p)\n    except (FileNotFoundError, ValueError): pass\n    try:  # CPU affinity (cpuset \u2014 used by RunPod, Docker --cpuset-cpus)\n        return len(os.sched_getaffinity(0))\n    except (AttributeError, OSError): pass\n    return os.cpu_count() or 1\n\ndef _container_ram_gb() -> float | None:\n    \"\"\"Read RAM limit from cgroup (containers/pods).\"\"\"\n    for p in [\"/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.max\", \"/sys/fs/cgroup/memory/memory.limit_in_bytes\"]:\n        try:\n            v = Path(p).read_text().strip()\n            if v != \"max\" and int(v) < 1_000_000_000_000:\n                return int(v) / 1e9\n        except (FileNotFoundError, ValueError): pass\n    return None\n\nNUM_CPUS = _detect_cpus()\nHAS_GPU = torch.cuda.is_available()\nVRAM_GB = torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_mem / 1e9 if HAS_GPU else 0\nDEVICE = torch.device(\"cuda\" if HAS_GPU else \"cpu\")\nTOTAL_RAM_GB = _container_ram_gb() or psutil.virtual_memory().total / 1e9\nAVAILABLE_RAM_GB = min(psutil.virtual_memory().available / 1e9, TOTAL_RAM_GB)\n```\n\n## Step 4 \u2014 Set Memory Limits\n\nOOM kills the entire container. **Every script MUST set RAM and VRAM limits at startup.**\n\nDecide the budget based on what the script actually needs. Estimate data size \u00d7 2-5x for in-memory overhead, then add ~50% breathing room for temporaries. You may use up to 90% of available RAM/VRAM, but **scale gradually** \u2014 start small (e.g. 30-50%), verify it works, then increase toward the limit. Never exceed 90% to keep a buffer for the OS, system processes, and the agent runtime itself. Going over crashes the container/machine with no recovery.\n\n```python\nimport resource, psutil\n\n_avail = psutil.virtual_memory().available\nRAM_BUDGET = ???  # YOU decide: estimate what this script needs (in bytes)\nassert RAM_BUDGET < _avail, f\"Budget {RAM_BUDGET/1e9:.1f}GB > available {_avail/1e9:.1f}GB\"\nresource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (RAM_BUDGET * 3, RAM_BUDGET * 3))  # 3x: virtual > RSS; raises MemoryError on exceed\n\nif HAS_GPU:\n    _free, _total = torch.cuda.mem_get_info(0)\n    VRAM_BUDGET = ???  # YOU decide: estimate GPU memory needs\n    torch.cuda.set_per_process_memory_fraction(min(VRAM_BUDGET / _total, 0.95))  # raises OutOfMemoryError on exceed\n```\n\n## Memory-Safe Data Processing\n\n- **One at a time**: load one large object \u2192 process \u2192 `del obj; gc.collect()` \u2192 next\n- **Load only what you need**: select specific tables/columns/rows, not entire databases\n- **Test small first**: run on a sample before scaling to full data to estimate memory/time\n- **Free intermediates in loops**: don't accumulate large results \u2014 aggregate incrementally\n- **Size before loading**: check file/dataset size before loading; if it's >30% of `RAM_BUDGET`, chunk it\n\n## Common Mistakes (from real crashes)\n\n- **Skipping this skill entirely** \u2014 loading data with no RAM detection, no limits, no budget. Container OOM-killed, all agents lost.\n- **Using `psutil.virtual_memory().total` instead of `_container_ram_gb()`** \u2014 reports host RAM (e.g. 66 GB) when container limit is 28 GB. You MUST use the cgroup-aware functions above.\n- **Loading all tables from a multi-table database at once** \u2014 one agent loaded 14 RelBench tables simultaneously, spiked past container limit.\n- **Setting no memory limits** \u2014 without `resource.setrlimit` (RAM) and `set_per_process_memory_fraction` (VRAM), a runaway script OOM-kills the container instead of raising a catchable error.\n- **Using `os.cpu_count()` directly** \u2014 returns host CPUs (e.g. 192) instead of container limit (e.g. 4) on RunPod/Docker. Always use `_detect_cpus()` above which checks cgroup quota \u2192 CPU affinity \u2192 `os.cpu_count()` in order.\n\n## Hardware Use\n\n- Keep these results in mind for ALL subsequent tasks \u2014 don't assume more than detected\n- GPU if available and parallelizable, multiprocessing if multiple CPUs\n- Push available resources to their full potential \u2014 don't leave hardware idle\n\n---\nThis skill is located at `/ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-use-hardware`. Any files it references (e.g. under `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`) are relative to that directory."}], "is_error": false, "skill_name": "aii-use-hardware", "kind": "InvokeSkillObservation"}

{"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "**ALWAYS parallelize. Sequential processing is unacceptable for any non-trivial workload.** A sequential script doing 1000 API calls takes hours and fails halfway. An async version finishes in minutes with proper error handling. ALWAYS ask: \"Can this run in parallel?\" \u2014 the answer is almost always yes.\n\nRead aii-use-hardware skill first \u2192 get `NUM_CPUS`, `HAS_GPU`, `VRAM_GB`, `device`. Set `NUM_WORKERS` proportional to available CPU capacity \u2014 check `psutil.cpu_percent(interval=1)` and scale accordingly (e.g. 30% used \u2192 use ~70% of cores).\n\n## Decision Tree (follow strictly)\n\n- **I/O-bound** (API calls, downloads, web, file reads) \u2192 `asyncio` + `aiohttp` with `Semaphore(NUM_WORKERS * 4)`. NEVER do sequential HTTP requests in a loop.\n- **CPU-bound, vectorizable** \u2192 GPU available: PyTorch on device / No GPU: NumPy vectorized ops. NEVER loop over array elements in Python.\n- **CPU-bound, independent items** \u2192 `ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=NUM_WORKERS)`. NEVER process items one-by-one when they're independent.\n- **Sequential** \u2192 only acceptable when items have data dependencies (each depends on the previous result).\n\n## GPU Rules\n\n- Use up to 90% of available VRAM \u2014 scale gradually (start small, increase after each successful run, keep 10% buffer)\n- Move to device \u2192 compute \u2192 move back: `torch.tensor(data, device=device)` \u2192 `.cpu().numpy()`\n- OOM fallback: catch `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError` \u2192 `empty_cache()` \u2192 halve batch size \u2192 retry on GPU. Keep reducing until it fits. Stay on GPU.\n- Batch large data: chunk it, `del batch` between iterations to free VRAM\n\n## Parallelism Rules\n\n- **CPU-bound**: `ProcessPoolExecutor` + `as_completed`, pre-allocate result list indexed by submission order\n- **I/O-bound**: `asyncio` + `aiohttp`, `Semaphore(NUM_WORKERS * 4)`, single shared `ClientSession`, `asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)`\n- Always add `tenacity` retries for transient failures, always set timeouts on HTTP requests\n- **CRITICAL \u2014 `ProcessPoolExecutor` start method**: Default `fork` deadlocks with loguru (and any threading library). ALWAYS pass `mp_context=multiprocessing.get_context(\"spawn\")` when constructing `ProcessPoolExecutor` in any script that uses loguru, threading, or async I/O. Example:\n  ```python\n  import multiprocessing as mp\n  from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor\n  with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=N, mp_context=mp.get_context(\"spawn\")) as pool:\n      ...\n  ```\n\n---\nThis skill is located at `/ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-parallel-computing`. Any files it references (e.g. under `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`) are relative to that directory."}], "is_error": false, "skill_name": "aii-parallel-computing", "kind": "InvokeSkillObservation"}

<artifact_plan>
id: gen_plan_experiment_1_idx1
type: experiment
title: 'VUCCA: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Conformal Cache Admission'
summary: >-
  Implement the fully corrected VUCCA method featuring Adaptive Conformal Inference (ACI), soft-sigmoid admission bias, and
  fine-grained semantic clustering, evaluated across stationary, shift, and cold-start regimes against LRU, TinyLFU, and ARC2.
runpod_compute_profile: cpu_light
implementation_pseudocode: "import numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.cluster import KMeans\nfrom scipy.special import expit as sigmoid\n\
  \n# --- Components ---\nclass CacheSimulator:\n    # Implements LRU, TinyLFU, ARC2\n    # TinyLFU uses a Count-Min Sketch\
  \ for frequency tracking\n    # ARC2 maintains T1, T2, B1, B2 lists\n\nclass VUCCAAdmission:\n    def __init__(self, tau_base=0.3,\
  \ beta=0.4, gamma=5.0, alpha=0.1, lambda_aci=0.01):\n        self.tau_base = tau_base\n        self.beta = beta\n      \
  \  self.gamma = gamma\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.lambda_aci = lambda_aci\n        self.cluster_stats = {}\n\
  \        self.quantiles = {}\n\n    def get_bias(self, cluster_id):\n        # Soft-Sigmoid: tau_c = tau_base + beta * sigmoid(gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n        u_c = self.cluster_stats[cluster_id]['half_width']\n        return self.tau_base + self.beta * sigmoid(self.gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n\n    def update_conformal(self, cluster_id, actual_miss_rate, predicted_miss_rate, timestamp, regime):\n\
  \        s = abs(predicted_miss_rate - actual_miss_rate)\n        if regime == 'shift':\n            # ACI: Exponentially\
  \ weighted update\n            # weight = exp(-lambda * (current_t - t_i))\n            # Update weighted quantile q_hat\n\
  \            pass\n        else:\n            # Split Conformal: Add to calibration set, compute quantile of s\n       \
  \     pass\n\n# --- Main Experiment Loop ---\n# 1. Load datasets from art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y (stationary, shift, cold_start)\n\
  # 2. Feature Enhancement (Optional):\n#    If budget allows, call OpenRouter to get CLIP embeddings for sample IDs\n#  \
  \  Else use provided 512-d embeddings\n# 3. Clustering:\n#    Run KMeans(K=64) -> Merge clusters with < 5 elements into\
  \ nearest neighbor\n# 4. Simulation Logic:\n#    for regime in regimes:\n#        for chunk in chunks:\n#            # Split\
  \ chunk: 20% Calibration, 80% Test\n#            cal_data, test_data = split(chunk)\n#            \n#            # Step\
  \ A: Calibration\n#            for item in cal_data:\n#                # Update miss rate estimates and conformal quantiles\n\
  #                vucca.update_conformal(item.cluster, ...)\n#            \n#            # Step B: Execution\n#         \
  \   for request in test_data:\n#                if request.item not in cache:\n#                    tau_c = vucca.get_bias(request.cluster)\n\
  #                    if admission_filter(request, tau_c):\n#                        cache.admit(request.item)\n#       \
  \         else:\n#                    cache.hit()\n#\n# 5. Ablations:\n#    - Replace soft-sigmoid with exp(u_c)\n#    -\
  \ Replace ACI with standard Split Conformal in 'shift' regime\n#    - Change K=64 to K=10\n#    - Replace Conformal Interval\
  \ Width with Entropy-based uncertainty\n#\n# 6. Metrics Calculation:\n#    - Hit Ratio = Hits / Total Requests\n#    - Throughput\
  \ = (Hits * Hit_Latency + Misses * Miss_Latency) / Total_Time\n#    - Coverage = count(actual_miss_rate in [M-q, M+q]) /\
  \ total_samples"
fallback_plan: >-
  If ACI convergence is too slow or unstable, fallback to a sliding-window Conformal Prediction where the calibration set
  is the last N requests. If the soft-sigmoid bias doesn't show significant improvement over baselines, test a piecewise-linear
  bias function. If CLIP API costs exceed $5, rely entirely on the synthetic 512-d embeddings provided in the dataset, as
  they are already designed to mimic semantic structure.
testing_plan: |-
  1. Mini-Run: Execute the pipeline on 1 chunk of the 'stationary' regime. Verify that hit rates for LRU/TinyLFU are consistent with known behaviors and that the conformal interval is effectively bounding the miss rate.
  2. Bias Verification: Print the `tau_c` values for high-confidence vs low-confidence clusters to ensure the soft-sigmoid is producing a gradient (e.g., 0.3 to 0.7) rather than binary values.
  3. Coverage Check: Calculate the percentage of actual miss rates that fall within the conformal intervals for the calibration set; it should be approximately 1-alpha (e.g., 90%).
  4. Regime Sanity: In the 'shift' regime, verify that ACI-based thresholds adapt faster than split-conformal thresholds after the popularity shift point.
</artifact_plan>

id: art_IuIwT_8rgp5S
type: research
title: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Cache Admission Survey
summary: >-
  Comprehensive survey across 20+ verified sources confirming: (A) 12 modern cache admission baselines identified with exact
  venues, mechanisms, and metrics — ZERO use conformal prediction or visual/semantic signals for admission; (B) Conformal
  prediction gap confirmed — no prior work applies distribution-free uncertainty quantification to cache admission, with the
  closest analogs being DEAP Cache's KDE (distribution modeling, not conformal) and Guard's robustness bounds (algorithmic,
  not statistical); (C) Visual/semantic embeddings used for similarity search in caching but never combined with uncertainty-aware
  admission; (D) Standard block traces lack semantic signal, requiring object/CDN traces (S3-FIFO collection: 6,594 traces,
  856B requests) or synthetic embedding-object workloads; (E) Key risks: exchangeability violations requiring ACI, embedding
  inference overhead, and cold-start handling.

--- Dependency 2 ---
id: art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y
type: dataset
title: Cache Access Trace Datasets with Visual Embeddings
summary: >-
  This artifact provides three cache-access trace datasets for evaluating conformal cache admission policies. Each dataset
  pairs real-derived request patterns (based on Tencent CDN trace statistics) with 512-d visual embeddings and semantic cluster
  metadata. The three regimes are: (1) stationary_hot_clusters — stable Zipf-distributed popularity with a few dominant hot
  clusters, (2) sudden_popularity_shift — request mass migrates from old clusters to new ones midway through the sequence,
  and (3) cold_start — a stream of previously-unseen items with no warm-up period. Each dataset contains ~800-900 items with
  512-d embeddings, popularity counts, cluster IDs, timestamps, and temporal train/validation/test splits. Total of 2,578
  examples across 3 datasets. The request sequences are ordered by timestamp (no future leakage), enabling downstream experiments
  to replay TinyLFU, ARC2, LRU, and new conformal admission policies. No hit/miss labels or admission decisions are pre-computed;
  those are downstream experiment outputs. Full dataset is 25MB, with mini (9 examples) and preview (9 examples, truncated)
  variants for quick inspection.
workspace_path: >-
  /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_XUVIPW24BQbP/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1
out_dependency_files:
  file_list:
  - data.py
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json
  data_file_paths:
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json

Data files come in three sizes:
- preview_*_out.json — READ THIS to inspect the data structure
- mini_*_out.json (~3 examples) — use for prototyping/testing
- full_*_out.json (complete) — use for the final production run. NEVER open it directly (too large to read into context). Instead, extract values programmatically with shell commands (e.g. grep) or a Python script (use aii-long-running-tasks skill for scripts).
</dependencies>

<artifact_plan>
id: gen_plan_experiment_1_idx1
type: experiment
title: 'VUCCA: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Conformal Cache Admission'
summary: >-
  Implement the fully corrected VUCCA method featuring Adaptive Conformal Inference (ACI), soft-sigmoid admission bias, and
  fine-grained semantic clustering, evaluated across stationary, shift, and cold-start regimes against LRU, TinyLFU, and ARC2.
runpod_compute_profile: cpu_light
implementation_pseudocode: "import numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.cluster import KMeans\nfrom scipy.special import expit as sigmoid\n\
  \n# --- Components ---\nclass CacheSimulator:\n    # Implements LRU, TinyLFU, ARC2\n    # TinyLFU uses a Count-Min Sketch\
  \ for frequency tracking\n    # ARC2 maintains T1, T2, B1, B2 lists\n\nclass VUCCAAdmission:\n    def __init__(self, tau_base=0.3,\
  \ beta=0.4, gamma=5.0, alpha=0.1, lambda_aci=0.01):\n        self.tau_base = tau_base\n        self.beta = beta\n      \
  \  self.gamma = gamma\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.lambda_aci = lambda_aci\n        self.cluster_stats = {}\n\
  \        self.quantiles = {}\n\n    def get_bias(self, cluster_id):\n        # Soft-Sigmoid: tau_c = tau_base + beta * sigmoid(gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n        u_c = self.cluster_stats[cluster_id]['half_width']\n        return self.tau_base + self.beta * sigmoid(self.gamma\
  \ * (1 - u_c))\n\n    def update_conformal(self, cluster_id, actual_miss_rate, predicted_miss_rate, timestamp, regime):\n\
  \        s = abs(predicted_miss_rate - actual_miss_rate)\n        if regime == 'shift':\n            # ACI: Exponentially\
  \ weighted update\n            # weight = exp(-lambda * (current_t - t_i))\n            # Update weighted quantile q_hat\n\
  \            pass\n        else:\n            # Split Conformal: Add to calibration set, compute quantile of s\n       \
  \     pass\n\n# --- Main Experiment Loop ---\n# 1. Load datasets from art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y (stationary, shift, cold_start)\n\
  # 2. Feature Enhancement (Optional):\n#    If budget allows, call OpenRouter to get CLIP embeddings for sample IDs\n#  \
  \  Else use provided 512-d embeddings\n# 3. Clustering:\n#    Run KMeans(K=64) -> Merge clusters with < 5 elements into\
  \ nearest neighbor\n# 4. Simulation Logic:\n#    for regime in regimes:\n#        for chunk in chunks:\n#            # Split\
  \ chunk: 20% Calibration, 80% Test\n#            cal_data, test_data = split(chunk)\n#            \n#            # Step\
  \ A: Calibration\n#            for item in cal_data:\n#                # Update miss rate estimates and conformal quantiles\n\
  #                vucca.update_conformal(item.cluster, ...)\n#            \n#            # Step B: Execution\n#         \
  \   for request in test_data:\n#                if request.item not in cache:\n#                    tau_c = vucca.get_bias(request.cluster)\n\
  #                    if admission_filter(request, tau_c):\n#                        cache.admit(request.item)\n#       \
  \         else:\n#                    cache.hit()\n#\n# 5. Ablations:\n#    - Replace soft-sigmoid with exp(u_c)\n#    -\
  \ Replace ACI with standard Split Conformal in 'shift' regime\n#    - Change K=64 to K=10\n#    - Replace Conformal Interval\
  \ Width with Entropy-based uncertainty\n#\n# 6. Metrics Calculation:\n#    - Hit Ratio = Hits / Total Requests\n#    - Throughput\
  \ = (Hits * Hit_Latency + Misses * Miss_Latency) / Total_Time\n#    - Coverage = count(actual_miss_rate in [M-q, M+q]) /\
  \ total_samples"
fallback_plan: >-
  If ACI convergence is too slow or unstable, fallback to a sliding-window Conformal Prediction where the calibration set
  is the last N requests. If the soft-sigmoid bias doesn't show significant improvement over baselines, test a piecewise-linear
  bias function. If CLIP API costs exceed $5, rely entirely on the synthetic 512-d embeddings provided in the dataset, as
  they are already designed to mimic semantic structure.
testing_plan: |-
  1. Mini-Run: Execute the pipeline on 1 chunk of the 'stationary' regime. Verify that hit rates for LRU/TinyLFU are consistent with known behaviors and that the conformal interval is effectively bounding the miss rate.
  2. Bias Verification: Print the `tau_c` values for high-confidence vs low-confidence clusters to ensure the soft-sigmoid is producing a gradient (e.g., 0.3 to 0.7) rather than binary values.
  3. Coverage Check: Calculate the percentage of actual miss rates that fall within the conformal intervals for the calibration set; it should be approximately 1-alpha (e.g., 90%).
  4. Regime Sanity: In the 'shift' regime, verify that ACI-based thresholds adapt faster than split-conformal thresholds after the popularity shift point.
</artifact_plan>

id: art_IuIwT_8rgp5S
type: research
title: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Cache Admission Survey
summary: >-
  Comprehensive survey across 20+ verified sources confirming: (A) 12 modern cache admission baselines identified with exact
  venues, mechanisms, and metrics — ZERO use conformal prediction or visual/semantic signals for admission; (B) Conformal
  prediction gap confirmed — no prior work applies distribution-free uncertainty quantification to cache admission, with the
  closest analogs being DEAP Cache's KDE (distribution modeling, not conformal) and Guard's robustness bounds (algorithmic,
  not statistical); (C) Visual/semantic embeddings used for similarity search in caching but never combined with uncertainty-aware
  admission; (D) Standard block traces lack semantic signal, requiring object/CDN traces (S3-FIFO collection: 6,594 traces,
  856B requests) or synthetic embedding-object workloads; (E) Key risks: exchangeability violations requiring ACI, embedding
  inference overhead, and cold-start handling.

--- Dependency 2 ---
id: art_yLa3dYMVqH-Y
type: dataset
title: Cache Access Trace Datasets with Visual Embeddings
summary: >-
  This artifact provides three cache-access trace datasets for evaluating conformal cache admission policies. Each dataset
  pairs real-derived request patterns (based on Tencent CDN trace statistics) with 512-d visual embeddings and semantic cluster
  metadata. The three regimes are: (1) stationary_hot_clusters — stable Zipf-distributed popularity with a few dominant hot
  clusters, (2) sudden_popularity_shift — request mass migrates from old clusters to new ones midway through the sequence,
  and (3) cold_start — a stream of previously-unseen items with no warm-up period. Each dataset contains ~800-900 items with
  512-d embeddings, popularity counts, cluster IDs, timestamps, and temporal train/validation/test splits. Total of 2,578
  examples across 3 datasets. The request sequences are ordered by timestamp (no future leakage), enabling downstream experiments
  to replay TinyLFU, ARC2, LRU, and new conformal admission policies. No hit/miss labels or admission decisions are pre-computed;
  those are downstream experiment outputs. Full dataset is 25MB, with mini (9 examples) and preview (9 examples, truncated)
  variants for quick inspection.
workspace_path: >-
  /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_XUVIPW24BQbP/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1
out_dependency_files:
  file_list:
  - data.py
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json
  data_file_paths:
  - full_data_out.json
  - mini_data_out.json
  - preview_data_out.json

Data files come in three sizes:
- preview_*_out.json — READ THIS to inspect the data structure
- mini_*_out.json (~3 examples) — use for prototyping/testing
- full_*_out.json (complete) — use for the final production run. NEVER open it directly (too large to read into context). Instead, extract values programmatically with shell commands (e.g. grep) or a Python script (use aii-long-running-tasks skill for scripts).
</dependencies>

Use `uv` for ALL package operations:\n  ```bash\n  uv venv .venv --python=3.12\n  source .venv/bin/activate  # or: .venv/bin/python script.py\n  uv pip install pandas loguru  # NOT: pip install\n  ```\n- Create `.toml` file with dependencies, create uv `.venv` and activate it\n- NO inline dependencies (no `# /// script` headers)\n\n## Logging\n\nUse `loguru` for all logging. Add a file sink alongside stdout.\n\n```python\nfrom loguru import logger\nimport sys\n\nlogger.remove()  # Remove default handler\nlogger.add(sys.stdout, level=\"INFO\", format=\"{time:HH:mm:ss}|{level:<7}|{message}\")\nlogger.add(\"logs/run.log\", rotation=\"30 MB\", level=\"DEBUG\")\n```\n\nRules:\n- Log every major step (data loading, processing start/end, results)\n- If applicable, log every LLM API call input and output\n- Truncate long outputs in logs (add truncation logic for potentially large strings)\n- Use `logger.error()` in except blocks (traceback auto-captured)\n\n## Error Handling\n\n- Wrap major operations in try/except blocks\n- Use `@logger.catch(reraise=True)` decorator on main functions \u2014 without `reraise=True`, the script exits 0 even on uncaught exceptions, hiding failures from downstream consumers\n- Use explicit exception types, not bare `except:`\n- Never silently swallow exceptions \u2014 always log them\n\n```python\n@logger.catch(reraise=True)\ndef main():\n    try:\n        data = load_data(path)\n    except FileNotFoundError:\n        logger.error(\"Data file not found\")\n        raise\n    except json.JSONDecodeError:\n        logger.error(\"Invalid JSON in data file\")\n        raise\n```\n\n## Code Structure\n\n- Use `pathlib.Path` for file operations: `Path(\"data/input.json\").read_text()` not `open(...).read()`\n- Use type hints for function signatures\n- Use keyword arguments for functions with more than 4 parameters\n- No hardcoded paths \u2014 derive from script location or accept as arguments\n\n## Script Pattern\n\nStandard pattern for experiment/evaluation scripts:\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Brief description of what this script does.\"\"\"\n\nfrom loguru import logger\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json\nimport sys\n\nlogger.remove()\nlogger.add(sys.stdout, level=\"INFO\", format=\"{time:HH:mm:ss}|{level:<7}|{message}\")\nlogger.add(\"logs/run.log\", rotation=\"30 MB\", level=\"DEBUG\")\n\n@logger.catch(reraise=True)\ndef main():\n    # Load data\n    data_path = Path(\"full_data_out.json\")\n    logger.info(f\"Loading data from {data_path}\")\n    data = json.loads(data_path.read_text())\n    logger.info(f\"Loaded {len(data['examples'])} examples\")\n\n    # Process\n    results = []\n    for i, example in enumerate(data[\"examples\"]):\n        try:\n            result = process(example)\n            results.append(result)\n        except Exception:\n            logger.error(f\"Failed on example {i}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Save output\n    output = {\"examples\": results}\n    Path(\"method_out.json\").write_text(json.dumps(output, {"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "## Core Principles\n\n1. **Time budget first**: Read your time/runtime constraints before running anything. Set every Bash timeout to fit within the budget.\n2. **Start small, scale up**: Run on minimal input first, fix errors, then increase scale.\n3. **Extrapolate before scaling**: Use recorded runtimes to predict whether the next step fits in the budget. Don't guess \u2014 calculate.\n4. **Background execution**: For anything that takes >1 min, run in background (`run_in_background=true`) and do useful work while waiting.\n5. **Stop early if needed**: Quality results on less data beats a timeout or crash. It's always acceptable to stop at a smaller scale.\n\n---\n\n## Gradual Scaling Sequence\n\nRun code at increasing data sizes, checking runtime at each step.\n\nSubstitute your actual file names:\n- `{mini_file}` \u2014 mini JSON (3 examples) from dependency workspace\n- `{full_file}` \u2014 full dataset from dependency workspace\n- `{script}` \u2014 your processing script (e.g., `./method.py`, `./eval.py`)\n- `{schema}` \u2014 JSON schema to validate output against\n\n**STEP 1 \u2014 MINI DATA:** Run `{script}` on `{mini_file}`. Do NOT truncate logs. Fix all errors. Validate output against `{schema}`. Verify you are NOT using mock scripts, mock data, or mock APIs.\n\n**STEP 2 \u2014 10 EXAMPLES:** Modify `{script}` to load only the first 10 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Validate schema. Record the runtime.\n\n**STEP 3 \u2014 50 EXAMPLES:** Load first 50 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Record runtime. **EXTRAPOLATE**: Using runtimes from steps 2-3, estimate time per example. Calculate how many examples fit in your remaining time budget. If 50 already used most of the budget, stop here.\n\n**STEP 4 \u2014 100 EXAMPLES (if budget allows):** Load first 100 examples. Run and fix errors. Record runtime. Re-extrapolate with the new data point.\n\n**STEP 5 \u2014 200 EXAMPLES (if budget allows):** Load first 200 examples from `{full_file}`. Run and fix errors. Record runtime.\n\n**STEP 6 \u2014 MAXIMIZE:** Using all recorded runtimes, extrapolate time-per-example (it may not be perfectly linear \u2014 account for overhead). Calculate the maximum number of examples that fits within your remaining time budget with a 10% safety margin. Load that many (or all if they fit). Run and validate.\n\n## Final Testing Phase\n\nAfter completing the scaling sequence, redo the entire sequence **one more time** up to your final example count:\n\nmini \u2192 10 \u2192 50 \u2192 100 \u2192 200 \u2192 max\n\nAt each scale: look for issues, fix problems, validate output, ensure it completes within time limits.\n\n---\n\n## Background Execution\n\nFor any step that takes >1 min, run as a **background task**:\n\n1. Launch with Bash `run_in_background=true`\n2. While it runs, use the time productively:\n   - Sanity-check previous outputs\n   - Verify file integrity (correct field names, non-empty values)\n   - Review code for edge cases at larger scale\n   - Prepare the next step\n3. Check back on the background task to get results\n4. If it failed, fix errors and re-run\n\n---\n\n## Resource Limits\n\nSet hard RAM and CPU time limits so code fails fast instead of crashing the system. Read limits from `<hardware>` and leave headroom for the OS (e.g., if 16GB total, cap at 14GB).\n\nPython example using stdlib `resource` module:\n```python\nimport resource\nresource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (14 * 1024**3, 14 * 1024**3))  # 14GB RAM\nresource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_CPU, (3600, 3600))  # 1 hour CPU time\n```\nExceeding RAM raises `MemoryError`. Exceeding CPU time sends `SIGKILL`.\n\n## Monitoring\n\nAt each step, record runtime AND check resource usage (`free -h` for RAM, `top -bn1 | head -5` for CPU). If memory usage is climbing toward the limit or CPU is pegged, stop and investigate before scaling further.\n\n---\nThis skill is located at `/ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-long-running-tasks`. Any files it references (e.g. under `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`) are relative to that directory."}], "is_error": false, "skill_name": "aii-long-running-tasks", "kind": "InvokeSkillObservation"}

exp_eval_sol_out --file /tmp/eval_out.json\n```\n\n**Parallel execution (multiple validations):**\n\nIMPORTANT: When validating multiple files, use GNU parallel instead of separate Bash tool calls:\n```bash\nexport SKILL_DIR=\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo /ai-inventor)/.claude/skills/aii-json\" && \\\nexport PY=\"$SKILL_DIR/../.ability_client_venv/bin/python\" && \\\nexport S=\"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/aii_json_validate_schema.py\" && \\\nparallel -j 50 -k --group --will-cite '$PY $S --format {1} --file {2}' ::: 'exp_sel_data_out' 'exp_gen_sol_out' 'exp_eval_sol_out' :::+ '/tmp/full_data_out.json' '/tmp/method_out.json' '/tmp/eval_out.json'\n```\n\n**Example output (success):**\n```\nValidating: aii_json_validate_schema.py\nFormat: {"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "**ALWAYS parallelize. Sequential processing is unacceptable for any non-trivial workload.** A sequential script doing 1000 API calls takes hours and fails halfway. An async version finishes in minutes with proper error handling. ALWAYS ask: \"Can this run in parallel?\" \u2014 the answer is almost always yes.\n\nRead aii-use-hardware skill first \u2192 get `NUM_CPUS`, `HAS_GPU`, `VRAM_GB`, `device`. Set `NUM_WORKERS` proportional to available CPU capacity \u2014 check `psutil.cpu_percent(interval=1)` and scale accordingly (e.g. 30% used \u2192 use ~70% of cores).\n\n## Decision Tree (follow strictly)\n\n- **I/O-bound** (API calls, downloads, web, file reads) \u2192 `asyncio` + `aiohttp` with `Semaphore(NUM_WORKERS * 4)`. NEVER do sequential HTTP requests in a loop.\n- **CPU-bound, vectorizable** \u2192 GPU available: PyTorch on device / No GPU: NumPy vectorized ops. NEVER loop over array elements in Python.\n- **CPU-bound, independent items** \u2192 `ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=NUM_WORKERS)`. NEVER process items one-by-one when they're independent.\n- **Sequential** \u2192 only acceptable when items have data dependencies (each depends on the previous result).\n\n## GPU Rules\n\n- Use up to 90% of available VRAM \u2014 scale gradually (start small, increase after each successful run, keep 10% buffer)\n- Move to device \u2192 compute \u2192 move back: `torch.tensor(data, device=device)` \u2192 `.cpu().numpy()`\n- OOM fallback: catch `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError` \u2192 `empty_cache()` \u2192 halve batch size \u2192 retry on GPU. Keep reducing until it fits. Stay on GPU.\n- Batch large data: chunk it, `del batch` between iterations to free VRAM\n\n## Parallelism Rules\n\n- **CPU-bound**: `ProcessPoolExecutor` + `as_completed`, pre-allocate result list indexed by submission order\n- **I/O-bound**: `asyncio` + `aiohttp`, `Semaphore(NUM_WORKERS * 4)`, single shared `ClientSession`, `asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)`\n- Always add `tenacity` retries for transient failures, always set timeouts on HTTP requests\n- **CRITICAL \u2014 `ProcessPoolExecutor` start method**: Default `fork` deadlocks with loguru (and any threading library). ALWAYS pass `mp_context=multiprocessing.get_context(\"spawn\")` when constructing `ProcessPoolExecutor` in any script that uses loguru, threading, or async I/O. Example:\n  ```python\n  import multiprocessing as mp\n  from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor\n  with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=N, mp_context=mp.get_context(\"spawn\")) as pool:\n      ...\n  ```\n\n---\nThis skill is located at `/ai-inventor/.claude/skills/aii-parallel-computing`. Any files it references (e.g. under `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`) are relative to that directory."}], "is_error": false, "skill_name": "aii-parallel-computing", "kind": "InvokeSkillObservation"}

{"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_XUVIPW24BQbP/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_research_1/research_out.json:\n     1\t{\n     2\t  \"title\": \"Visual Uncertainty-Aware Cache Admission: Verified Survey of Policies, Conformal Gap, and Embedding Caching\",\n     3\t  \"summary\": \"Comprehensive survey across 20+ verified sources confirming: (A) 12 modern cache admission baselines identified with exact venues, mechanisms, and metrics \u2014 ZERO use conformal prediction or visual/semantic signals for admission; (B) Conformal prediction gap confirmed \u2014 no prior work applies distribution-free uncertainty quantification to cache admission, with the closest analogs being DEAP Cache's KDE (distribution modeling, not conformal) and Guard's robustness bounds (algorithmic, not statistical); (C) Visual/semantic embeddings used for similarity search in caching but never combined with uncertainty-aware admission; (D) Standard block traces lack semantic signal, requiring object/CDN traces (S3-FIFO collection: 6,594 traces, 856B requests) or synthetic embedding-object workloads; (E) Key risks: exchangeability violations requiring ACI, embedding inference overhead, and cold-start handling.\",\n     4\t  \"answer\": \"This survey systematically investigated three research fronts to validate the novelty and feasibility of visual-uncertainty-aware cache admission.\\n\\n**FRONT A: Modern Cache Admission Baselines (12 Verified Methods)**\\nWe identified and verified 12 state-of-the-art cache admission/eviction policies spanning 2017\u20132026. The most significant are: (1) **TinyLFU** (ACM TOCS 2017) uses a frequency sketch for admission under skewed distributions [1]; (2) **CACHEUS** (USENIX FAST '21) employs learning-based replacement with LFU, LIRS, ARC, SR-LRU, and CR-LFU as experts across 329 workloads [2]; (3) **S3-FIFO** (SOSP '23) uses three static FIFO queues with a small filter queue to block one-hit wonders, evaluated on 6,594 traces from 14 datasets (Twitter, Meta, Microsoft, Wikimedia, Tencent, Alibaba, major CDNs) totaling 856 billion requests, achieving 6\u00d7 higher throughput than optimized LRU [3]; (4) **Segcache** (USENIX NSDI '21) uses TTL-based segment caching, achieving 22\u201360% less memory than state-of-the-art [4]; (5) **CacheSack** (USENIX ATC '22) applies knapsack optimization for Google datacenter flash cache admission, improving operational cost by 6.5% [5]; (6) **DEAP Cache** (arXiv:2009.09206) is the most ML-heavy approach, using end-to-end deep learning for eviction+admission+prefetching with Kernel Density Estimation for non-stationary data [6]; (7) **Guard** (NeurIPS '25, arXiv:2507.16242) provides robustification of learning-augmented caching with O(1) overhead [7]; (8) **AdCache** (EDBT '26) uses RL for LSM-tree cache partitioning [8]; (9) **Chameleon** (MICRO '25, arXiv:2411.17741) caches LoRA adapters for LLM inference [9]; (10) **Bandit Learning-to-Cache** (arXiv:2004.00472) frames caching as regret minimization [10]; (11) **Learning-Augmented Caching** (arXiv:2410.01760) provides competitive ratio bounds with predictions [11]; and (12) **LeCaR** (HotStorage '18) improves ARC-ALRU via ML [12].\\n\\n**Critical finding**: ZERO of these 12 baselines apply distribution-free uncertainty quantification (conformal prediction or equivalent) to admission decisions. ZERO use visual/semantic content signals for admission. DEAP Cache's KDE is distribution modeling, not conformal prediction with coverage guarantees. Guard's robustness bounds are algorithmic, not statistical uncertainty quantification.\\n\\n**FRONT B: Conformal Prediction + Caching Gap (Confirmed)**\\nWe searched extensively for any prior work applying conformal prediction to cache admission and found none. The closest matches are: (1) \\\"Efficient Conformal Prediction via Cascaded Inference with Expanded Admission\\\" (arXiv:2007.03114, ICLR '21) uses \\\"admission\\\" in the ML classification sense (admissible answer candidates), NOT cache admission [13]; (2) DEAP Cache uses KDE for non-stationary data modeling but provides no coverage guarantees [6]; (3) Guard provides robustness bounds for learning-augmented caching but via algorithmic analysis, not conformal prediction [7]; (4) Sequential Predictive Conformal Inference for Time Series (arXiv:2212.03463) addresses time series forecasting, not caching [14].\\n\\n**The gap is clear and significant**: No prior work generates prediction intervals for item popularity/future access with coverage guarantees, uses interval widths as uncertainty signals for admission bias, or applies distribution-free uncertainty quantification to determine admission confidence. This represents a novel research direction.\\n\\n**FRONT C: Visual/Semantic Embeddings in Caching**\\nVisual/semantic embeddings are widely used for similarity-based retrieval in caching (CLIP, ViT, foundation model embeddings), but NEVER combined with uncertainty-aware admission. Semantic caching for LLMs uses cosine similarity thresholds for cache hits [15]. CLIP-powered multi-modal search uses Redis vector indexing for retrieval [16]. IGTCache uses ViT for image retrieval, achieving +55.6% hit ratio, but without uncertainty integration [17]. Prompt caching caches LLM prompt embeddings but is domain-specific [18]. Document embedding caches implement metric indices for nearest-neighbor queries [19]. None incorporate embedding-derived uncertainty into admission decisions.\\n\\n**DATASET FEASIBILITY**: Standard block I/O traces (MSR Cambridge, CloudPhysics/Tencent) contain NO semantic or visual signal. The S3-FIFO collection provides 6,594 traces from 14 datasets including Wikimedia, Twitter, and CDN object traces with URL-level granularity \u2014 these are the best available for visual content mapping. Synthetic embedding-object workload generators are also needed for controlled experiments.\\n\\n**KEY RISKS**: (1) Exchangeability violations under popularity shifts likely require adaptive conformal inference (ACI) rather than split conformal; (2) Embedding inference overhead must be profiled against hit ratio gains; (3) Cold-start items lack history for uncertainty estimation.\\n\\n**CONFIDENCE**: High confidence in the novelty gap (conformal + cache admission = zero prior work). Medium confidence on trace availability (S3-FIFO collection exists but download access needs verification). Medium confidence on ACI necessity (depends on drift severity in target workloads).\",\n     5\t  \"sources\": [\n     6\t    {\n     7\t      \"index\": 1,\n     8\t      \"url\": \"http://www.diag.uniroma1.it/en/node/7244\",\n     9\t      \"title\": \"TinyLFU: A Highly Efficient Cache Admission Policy (ACM TOCS 2017)\",\n    10\t      \"summary\": \"Frequency-based cache admission using frequency sketch (Bloom filter variant). No uncertainty quantification or semantic signals. 64\nMIN_CLUSTER_SIZE = 5\nNUM_RUNS = 5  # For variance estimation\n\n\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500 Data Loading \u2500\u2500\u2500\ndef load_dataset(data_path: Path, max_examples: Optional[int] = None) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Load examples from a dataset file, optionally limiting count.\"\"\"\n    with open(data_path, 'r') as f:\n        raw = json.load(f)\n    \n    all_examples = []\n max_examples:\n                return all_examples\n    return all_examples\n\n\ndef load_all_regimes(max_examples_per_regime: Optional[int] = None) -> Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]:\n    \"\"\"Load all three regimes from the full dataset.\"\"\"\n    data_path = DATASET_DIR / \"full_data_out.json\"\n    if max_examples_per_regime:\n        data_path = DATASET_DIR / \"mini_data_out.json\"\n    \n    with open(data_path, 'r') as f:\n        raw = json.load(f)\n    \n    regimes = {}\n    for ds in raw.get(\"datasets\", []):\n        name = ds[\"dataset\"]\n        examples = []\n        for ex np.array(inp[\"embedding\"], dtype=np.float32),\n                \"popularity_count\": int(inp[\"popularity_count\"]),\n                \"cluster_id\": int(inp[\"cluster_id\"]),\n                \"timestamp\": int(inp[\"timestamp\"]),\n                \"image_file\": inp.get(\"image_file\", \"\"),\n                \"fold\": ex.get(\"metadata_fold\", 0),\n                \"dataset\": name,\n            })\n        if max_examples_per_regime:\n            examples = examples[:max_examples_per_regime]\n        regimes[name] = examples\n    return regimes\n\n\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500 Cache Simulators \u2500\u2500\u2500\nclass LRUCache:\n    \"\"\"Standard LRU cache with no admission filter (admits everything).\"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, capacity: int):\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.cache = OrderedDict()\n        self.hits = 0\n        self.misses = 0\n        self.total = 0\n\n    def access(self, item_id: int) -> bool:\n        self.total += 1\n        if item_id in self.cache:\n            self.cache.move_to_end(item_id)\n            self.hits += 1\n            return True\n        else:\n            self.misses += 1\n            if len(self.cache) >= self.capacity:\n                self.cache.popitem(last=False)\n            self.cache[item_id] = True\n            return False\n\n    @property\n    def hit_rate(self) -> float:\n        return self.hits / max(self.total, 1)\n\n\nclass TinyLFUCache:\n    \"\"\"TinyLFU: Frequency sketch-based admission filter + LRU.\"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, capacity: int, sketch_size: int = 4096):\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.cache = OrderedDict()\n        self.sketch_size = sketch_size\n        self.sketch = np.zeros((4, sketch_size), dtype=np.uint16)\n        self.hits = 0\n        self.misses = 0\n        self.total = 0\n\n    def _hashes(self, item_id: int) -> List[int]:\n        h = hash(item_id)\n        return [(h >> (8 * i)) % self.sketch_size for i in range(4)]\n\n    def _frequency(self, item_id: int) -> int:\n        hs = self._hashes(item_id)\n        return min(self.sketch[i][h] for i, h in enumerate(hs))\n\n    def _increment(self, item_id: int):\n        hs = self._hashes(item_id)\n        for i, h in enumerate(hs):\n            self.sketch[i][h] = min(self.sketch[i][h] + 1, 65535)\n\n    def access(self, item_id: int) -> bool:\n        self.total += 1\n        if item_id in self.cache:\n            self.cache.move_to_end(item_id)\n            self._increment(item_id)\n            self.hits += 1\n            return True\n        else:\n            self.misses += 1\n            freq = self._frequency(item_id)\n            if freq > 0 or len(self.cache) < self.capacity:\n                if len(self.cache) >= self.capacity:\n                    self.cache.popitem(last=False)\n                self.cache[item_id] = True\n                self._increment(item_id)\n            return False\n\n    @property\n    def hit_rate(self) -> float:\n        return self.hits / max(self.total, 1)\n\n\nclass ARC2Cache:\n    \"\"\"ARC2: Adaptive Replacement Cache with T1, T2, B1, B2 lists.\"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, capacity: int):\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.p = 0  # Replacement target\n        self.t1 = OrderedDict()  # Recent\n        self.t2 = OrderedDict()  # Frequent\n        self.b1 = OrderedDict()  # Ghost recent\n        self.b2 = OrderedDict()  # Ghost frequent\n        self.hits = 0\n        self.misses = 0\n        self.total = 0\n\n    def _adjust_p(self, in_b1: bool, in_b2: bool):\n        if in_b1:\n            self.p = min(self.p + len(self.t2), self.capacity)\n        elif in_b2:\n            self.p = max(self.p - len(self.t1), 0)\n\n    def access(self, item_id: int) -> bool:\n        self.total += 1\n        if item_id in self.t1:\n            self.t1.move_to_end(item_id)\n            del self.t1[item_id]\n            self.t2[item_id] = True\n            self.t2.move_to_end(item_id)\n            self.hits += 1\n            return True\n        if item_id in self.t2:\n            self.t2.move_to_end(item_id)\n            self.hits += 1\n            return True\n        if item_id in self.b1:\n            self._adjust_p(True, False)\n            self._evict(item_id, from_ghost=True, ghost_list='b1')\n            return False\n        if item_id in self.b2:\n            self._adjust_p(False, True)\n            self._evict(item_id, from_ghost=True, ghost_list='b2')\n            return False\n        self._evict(item_id, from_ghost=False)\n        self.misses += 1\n        return False\n\n    def _evict(self, new_item: int, from_ghost: bool = False, ghost_list: str = ''):\n        if not from_ghost:\n            if len(self.t1) + len(self.t2) >= self.capacity:\n                if len(self.t1) > self.p:\n                    evicted = next(iter(self.t1))\n                    del self.t1[evicted]\n                    self.b1[evicted] = True\n                else:\n                    evicted = next(iter(self.t2))\n                    del self.t2[evicted]\n                    self.b2[evicted] = True\n            # Limit ghost sizes\n            while len(self.b1) > self.capacity:\n                self.b1.popitem(last=False)\n            while len(self.b2) > self.capacity:\n                self.b2.popitem(last=False)\n        self.t1[new_item] = True\n        self.t1.move_to_end(new_item)\n\n    @property\n    def hit_rate(self) -> float:\n        return self.hits / max(self.total, 1)\n\n\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500 VUCCA Components \u2500\u2500\u2500\nclass ConformalTracker:\n    \"\"\"Tracks conformal prediction residuals per cluster.\"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, alpha: float = 0.1, lambda_aci: float = 0.01):\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.lambda_aci = lambda_aci\n        self.residuals: Dict[int, List[Tuple[float, float]]] = defaultdict(list)  # cluster -> [(residual, timestamp)]\n        self.quantiles: Dict[int, float] = {}\n        self.aci_weights: Dict[int, float] = {}  # Running weighted quantile for ACI\n\n    def add_residual(self, cluster_id: int, residual: float, timestamp: float, regime: str):\n        self.residuals[cluster_id].append((residual, timestamp))\n        if regime == \"sudden_popularity_shift\":\n            self._update_aci(cluster_id, timestamp)\n        else:\n            self._update_split_conformal(cluster_id)\n\n    def _update_split_conformal(self, cluster_id: int):\n        residuals = [r for r, _ in self.residuals[cluster_id]]\n        if len(residuals) >= 2:\n            q_level = np.ceil((1 - self.alpha) * (len(residuals) + 1)) / len(residuals)\n            q_level = min(q_level, 1.0)\n            self.quantiles[cluster_id] = np.quantile(residuals, q_level)\n\n    def _update_aci(self, cluster_id: int, current_time: float):\n        \"\"\"Adaptive Conformal Inference with exponential weighting.\"\"\"\n        items = self.residuals[cluster_id]\n        if len(items) < 2:\n            return\n        weights = []\n        residuals = []\n        for r, t in items:\n            w = math.exp(-self.lambda_aci * (current_time - t))\n            weights.append(w)\n            residuals.append(r)\n        weights = np.array(weights)\n        weights /= weights.sum()\n        residuals = np.array(residuals)\n        # Weighted quantile\n        sorted_idx = np.argsort(residuals)\n        sorted_r = residuals[sorted_idx]\n        sorted_w = weights[sorted_idx]\n        cum_w = np.cumsum(sorted_w)\n        q_level = 1 - self.alpha\n        idx = np.searchsorted(cum_w, q_level)\n        idx = min(idx, len(sorted_r) - 1)\n        self.quantiles[cluster_id] = sorted_r[idx]\n\n    def get_quantile(self, cluster_id: int) -> float:\n        return self.quantiles.get(cluster_id, 1.0)  # Default to wide interval\n\n    def get_half_width(self, cluster_id: int) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Get the conformal interval half-width as uncertainty measure.\"\"\"\n        q = self.get_quantile(cluster_id)\n        return max(q, 0.01)\n\n\nclass VUCCAAdmission:\n    \"\"\"VUCCA: Visual Uncertainty-Aware Conformal Cache Admission.\"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, tau_base: float = 0.3, beta: float = 0.4,\n                 gamma: float = 5.0, alpha: float = 0.1, lambda_aci: float = 0.01,\n                 use_soft_sigmoid: bool = True, use_aci: bool = True,\n                 k_clusters: int = 64, use_entropy: bool = False):\n        self.tau_base = tau_base\n        self.beta = beta\n        self.gamma = gamma\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.use_soft_sigmoid = use_soft_sigmoid\n        self.use_aci = use_aci\n        self.k_clusters = k_clusters\n        self.use_entropy = use_entropy\n        self.conformal = ConformalTracker(alpha=alpha, lambda_aci=lambda_aci)\n        self.cluster_stats: Dict[int, Dict[str, float]] = {}\n        self.cluster_embeddings: Dict[int, List[np.ndarray]] = defaultdict(list)\n        self.cluster_counts: Dict[int, int] = defaultdict(int)\n        self.cluster_total_requests: Dict[int, int] = defaultdict(int)\n        self.cluster_misses: Dict[int, int] = defaultdict(int)\n        self.kmeans = None\n        self.admission_decisions: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n    def fit_clusters(self, embeddings: np.ndarray):\n        \"\"\"Run KMeans clustering on embeddings.\"\"\"\n        n_samples = len(embeddings)\n        k = min(self.k_clusters, max(n_samples // 2, 1))\n        if k < 1:\n            k = 1\n        self.kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=k, random_state=42, n_init=10, max_iter=100)\n        labels = self.kmeans.fit_predict(embeddings)\n        # Merge small clusters\n        from collections import Counter\n        counts = Counter(labels)\n        merge_map = {}\n        for lbl, cnt in counts.items():\n            if cnt < MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE and len(counts) > 1:\n                # Find nearest larger cluster\n                center = self.kmeans.cluster_centers_[lbl]\n                best_dist = float('inf')\n                best_target = None\n                for other_lbl, other_cnt in counts.items():\n                    if other_cnt >= MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE and other_lbl != lbl:\n                        d = np.linalg.norm(center - self.kmeans.cluster_centers_[other_lbl])\n                        if d < best_dist:\n                            best_dist = d\n                            best_target = other_lbl\n                if best_target is not None:\n                    merge_map[lbl] = best_target\n                else:\n                    merge_map[lbl] = lbl\n            else:\n                merge_map[lbl] = lbl\n        return np.array([merge_map[l] for l in labels]), k\n\n    def get_bias(self, cluster_id: int) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Compute admission bias for a cluster.\"\"\"\n        half_width = self.conformal.get_half_width(cluster_id)\n        if self.use_entropy:\n            # Use entropy-based uncertainty instead\n            count = self.cluster_counts.get(cluster_id, 0)\n            if count > 1:\n                entropy = np.log(max(count, 1))\n                half_width = entropy / max(np.log(self.k_clusters), 1)\n        \n        if self.use_soft_sigmoid:\n            tau_c = self.tau_base + self.beta * sigmoid(self.gamma * (1 - half_width))\n        else:\n            # Exponential bias (ablation)\n            tau_c = self.tau_base + self.beta * math.exp(-half_width)\n            tau_c = min(tau_c, 1.0)\n        return tau_c\n\n    def admission_filter(self, item_id: int, cluster_id: int, popularity: int) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Decide whether to admit an item based on conformal uncertainty.\"\"\"\n        tau_c = self.get_bias(cluster_id)\n        # Higher popularity -> more likely to admit\n        # Use popularity as a score, compare against threshold\n        norm_pop = min(popularity / 1000.0, 1.0)  # Normalize popularity\n        score = norm_pop * tau_c + (1 - norm_pop) * 0.5\n        return score > 0.3  # Admission threshold\n\n    def update_from_calibration(self, item_id: int, cluster_id: int, popularity: int,\n                                timestamp: float, regime: str, was_miss: bool):\n        \"\"\"Update conformal statistics during calibration phase.\"\"\"\n        self.cluster_counts[cluster_id] += 1\n        self.cluster_total_requests[cluster_id] += 1\n        if was_miss:\n            self.cluster_misses[cluster_id] += 1\n        \n        # Estimate miss rate for this cluster\n        total = self.cluster_total_requests[cluster_id]\n        misses = self.cluster_misses[cluster_id]\n        actual_miss_rate = misses / max(total, 1)\n        predicted_miss_rate = 1.0 - min(popularity / 1000.0, 1.0)\n        \n        residual = abs(actual_miss_rate - predicted_miss_rate)\n        use_aci = self.use_aci and regime == \"sudden_popularity_shift\"\n        self.conformal.add_residual(cluster_id, residual, timestamp, \n                                     \"sudden_popularity_shift\" if use_aci else regime)\n\n\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500 Simulation Engine \u2500\u2500\u2500\ndef run_simulation(examples: List[Dict[str, Any]], cache_class, cache_kwargs: cache_class(**cache_kwargs)\n    total_requests = len(examples)\n    \n    # Split into calibration and test\n    cal_size = max(int(total_requests * CALIBRATION_FRACTION), 1)\n    cal_data = examples[:cal_size]\n    test_data = examples[cal_size:]\n    \n    # Phase 1: Calibration (warm-up)\n    if vucca is not None:\n        # Run calibration without VUCCA filtering to build stats\n        cal_cache = LRUCache(CACHE_SIZE)\n        for ex in cal_data:\n            hit = cal_cache.access(ex[\"item_id\"])\n            was_miss = not hit\n            vucca.update_from_calibration(\n                ex[\"item_id\"], ex[\"cluster_id\"], ex[\"popularity_count\"],\n                float(ex[\"timestamp\"]), regime, was_miss\n            )\n    \n    # Phase 2: Test\n    for ex in                cache.misses += 1\n                cache.total += 1\n        else:\n            cache.access(ex[\"item_id\"])\n    \n    # Compute metrics\n    hit_rate = cache.hit_rate\n    throughput total_checked = 0\n        for cluster_id in vucca.cluster_counts:\n            if vucca.cluster_counts[cluster_id] > 0:\n                q = vucca.conformal.get_quantile(cluster_id)\n                actual_mr = vucca.cluster_misses[cluster_id] / vucca.cluster_total_requests[cluster_id]\n                predicted_mr = 0.5  # Simplified\n                if abs(actual_mr - predicted_mr) <= q:\n                    covered += 1\n                total_checked += 1\n        coverage = covered / max(total_checked, 1)\n    \n    return {\n        \"hit_rate\": hit_rate,\n        \"throughput\":   \"test_size\": len(test_data),\n    }\n\n\ndef run_vucca_simulation(examples: List[Dict[str, Any]], vucca: VUCCAAdmission,\n                         regime: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Run test_data = examples[cal_size:]\n    \n    # Calibration phase\n    cal_cache = LRUCache(CACHE_SIZE)\n    for ex in cal_data:\n        hit = cal_cache.access(ex[\"item_id\"])\n        was_miss = not regimes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom loguru import logger\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport math\nimport resource\nimport gc\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.special import expit as
